## **3.3 Instructional Hours per Topic**

Utica Security Service, Inc. provides structured instructional hours to ensure adequate depth and comprehension:

* General Security Orientation: **8 hours**  
* Site-Specific Post Orders: **4 hours**  
* Customer Service & De-escalation: **4 hours**  
* Emergency Response Procedures: **4 hours**  
* Supervisor Training (supervisory roles): **6 hours**

*Note: For a complete breakdown of instructional hours allocated to each of the 15 required contract-specific training topics, detailed topic-by-topic hour allocations, and comprehensive training module descriptions, please refer to the attached **New Employee Training Program document,** **Part II, Section C:** Instructional Hours and Topic Allocation.*

Instructional hours may be expanded as required by post orders, site-specific conditions, or written directives issued by RGRTA. All training is delivered and overseen by the Utica Security Training Department to ensure continuous professional development and the sustained enhancement of security staff competencies in direct support of RGRTA's operational, safety, and customer service objectives.

## **3.4 Refresher and In-Service Training Frequency**

Utica Security Service, Inc. provides ongoing training to maintain proficiency and compliance:

* **Annual refresher training** for all officers  
* **Quarterly in-service training** addressing site-specific issues, policy updates, or identified trends  
* **Immediate remedial training** following incidents, complaints, or performance deficiencies

**Supervisory staff**  receive additional leadership and compliance training as needed.

*Note: Detailed curricula for annual in-service training, refresher modules, specialized training components, and quality assurance measures are provided in the attached In-Service Training Program document, which includes comprehensive coverage of all 10 in-service training modules, scenario-based refresher exercises, and ongoing specialized training requirements.*

## **Training Delivery Methods**

Training is delivered using multiple instructional methods to ensure comprehension and practical application:

* Instructor-led classroom instruction  
* Scenario-based discussions focused on transit-specific incidents  
* Case reviews drawn from public-facility environments  
* Knowledge checks and participation verification

Instructors are qualified and experienced in security operations within public-facing environments.

## **3.5 NYS DOS and DCJS Compliance**

Utica Security Service, Inc. ensures full compliance with all New York State licensing and training requirements, including:

* **New York State Security Guard Act of 1992** – All Security staff assigned to RGRTA facilities possess a valid New York State Security Guard Certification issued under the Act, or qualify for a law enforcement exemption as defined by the Act  
* **New York State Department of State (DOS)** security guard licensing and training requirements  
* **Division of Criminal Justice Services (DCJS)** standards and training mandates, where applicable  
* All applicable federal, state, and local regulations governing security services

**State-Mandated Training Requirements:**  
Utica Security Service, Inc. ensures all Security staff complete the following mandatory training as required by DOS and DCJS:

* **Eight (8) Hour Pre-Assignment Training** – Completed prior to assignment  
* **Sixteen (16) Hour On-the-Job Training (OJT)** – Completed within ninety (90) days of employment, as required by DCJS regulations  
* **Eight (8) Hour Annual In-Service Training** – Completed each calendar year to maintain current certifications and compliance

Only properly licensed and trained personnel are assigned to RGRTA facilities.

**Required Documentation and Recordkeeping:**  
Utica Security Service, Inc. maintains on file and makes available to RGRTA, upon request, the following compliance documentation for each Security staff member:

* A completed Form I-9, verifying employment eligibility in accordance with federal law  
* A copy of each Security staff member's current DOS-issued Photo ID card  
* A valid Certification of Compliance with DCJS training requirements

**Verification Process:**  
Utica Security Service, Inc. implements a systematic verification process to ensure ongoing compliance:

* **Pre-Assignment Verification:** All documentation, licenses, and certifications are verified prior to assignment to RGRTA facilities  
* **Periodic Compliance Audits:** Regular internal audits are conducted to verify that all Security staff maintain current licenses, certifications, and training compliance  
* **Documentation Maintenance:** All compliance documentation is securely maintained in individual personnel files and systematically tracked for renewal dates and training completion deadlines  
* **RGRTA Access:** All compliance documentation is readily accessible and made available to RGRTA upon request for review, audit, or verification purposes  
* **Training Record Integration:** Compliance documentation is integrated with training records (detailed in Section 3.6) to provide comprehensive oversight and verification of both regulatory compliance and contract-specific training requirements

This comprehensive compliance framework ensures alignment with regulatory and contract requirements, supporting the RFP scoring considerations for quality assurance in training delivery and documentation.

## **3.6 Training Recordkeeping and Reporting**

Utica Security Service, Inc. maintains **up-to-date, detailed training records for each Security staff member** assigned to RGRTA facilities. "Up-to-date" means that records are continuously updated immediately following training completion, certification renewal, or any training-related activity, ensuring that all records reflect current training status, certification validity, and compliance status at all times.

**3.6.1 Record Content and Documentation:**  
Individual employee training records include:

* Training topics completed with dates and instructional hours  
* Course topics and learning objectives  
* Certificates of completion  
* Instructor credentials and qualifications  
* Certification and license verification, including renewal dates and expiration tracking  
* Refresher and remedial training documentation with completion dates  
* Compliance documentation including:  
  * DOS-issued Photo ID card copies  
  * DCJS Certification of Compliance records  
  * Form I-9 employment eligibility verification (maintained in conjunction with training records)

**3.6.2 Record Organization and Storage:**  
Training records are organized and stored in a systematic, retrievable format:

* **Employee-Specific Files:** Each Security staff member has an individual personnel file containing all training records, certifications, and compliance documentation  
* **Electronic Recordkeeping System:** Records are maintained in a secure, searchable electronic database that enables efficient tracking, retrieval, and reporting  
* **File Organization:** Records are organized chronologically and by training category (pre-assignment, on-the-job, annual in-service, refresher, remedial) for easy access and verification  
* **Secure Storage:** All training records are securely maintained with appropriate access controls to protect employee privacy and ensure data integrity  
* **Retrieval Capability:** Records can be quickly retrieved by employee name, employee ID, training date, certification type, or compliance status to support RGRTA requests and internal audits

**3.6.3 Reporting and Availability:**  
Utica Security Service, Inc. provides training records to RGRTA in accordance with the following protocols:

* **Proactive Provision:** Training records are provided to RGRTA **as soon as they become available** following training completion, certification renewal, or upon initial assignment  
* **Upon Request:** All training records are readily accessible and made available to RGRTA immediately upon request, typically within one (1) business day for standard requests  
* **Reporting Format:** Records are provided in formats mutually acceptable to RGRTA, which may include electronic formats (PDF, spreadsheet, or database export) or hard copy, as requested  
* **Frequency:** Training records are updated and available on an ongoing basis; periodic summary reports may be provided at agreed-upon intervals or as requested by RGRTA  
* **Comprehensive Access:** RGRTA has access to both individual employee records and aggregate training completion reports to support oversight and compliance verification

**3.6.4 Quality Assurance and Verification:**  
Compliance audits are conducted periodically to ensure accuracy, completeness, and currency of all training records. The audit process includes:

* Verification of training completion against attendance records and certification documentation  
* Validation of certification expiration dates and renewal tracking  
* Cross-reference of training records with DOS and DCJS compliance documentation  
* Identification and remediation of any discrepancies or gaps in recordkeeping  
* Documentation of audit findings and corrective actions taken

This comprehensive recordkeeping and reporting system ensures alignment with regulatory requirements and supports the RFP scoring consideration for quality assurance in training delivery and documentation.

## **3.7 Quality Assurance & Continuous Improvement**

Training effectiveness is monitored through:

* Supervisor observations and performance feedback  
* Incident trend analysis  
* Review of reports and response outcomes  
* Identification of training gaps requiring remediation

When deficiencies are identified, targeted refresher or remedial training is implemented promptly to maintain performance standards

# **4\. Discipline and Complaint Handling Plan**

## **4.1 Policy Overview**

Utica Security Service, Inc. maintains a formal discipline and complaint handling program designed to ensure professional conduct, accountability, and prompt resolution of all incidents or allegations involving assigned personnel. All complaints are treated seriously and investigated in a fair, timely, and confidential manner. Our procedures are designed to protect the safety of the public, RGRTA personnel, and our officers while maintaining transparency and compliance with applicable laws and contractual requirements.

## **4.2 Complaint Intake and Reporting Procedures**

Complaints may be received through:

* RGRTA management or designated representatives  
* On-site supervisors  
* Written or verbal reports from members of the public  
* Internal reporting by employees

All complaints are documented immediately and escalated to management for review. RGRTA is notified promptly upon receipt of any complaint involving assigned personnel.

## **4.3 Handling of Specific Incident Types**

Utica Security Service, Inc. maintains detailed procedures for handling complaints in each of the following categories. Each category includes specific timelines, interim measures, communication protocols, corrective action determination processes, and reinstatement or replacement procedures.

### **a. Sexual Harassment**

**1\. Initial Response Timeline**

* All sexual harassment complaints are acknowledged within one (1) business day of receipt  
* RGRTA is notified immediately (within 2 hours) upon receipt of any sexual harassment complaint involving assigned personnel  
* Written acknowledgment is provided to the complainant within 24 hours, confirming receipt and outlining the investigation process  
* The accused employee is immediately removed from the RGRTA assignment pending investigation

**2\. Investigation Timeline**

* Initial status update provided to RGRTA within three (3) business days of complaint receipt  
* Full investigation is completed within ten (10) business days, barring exceptional circumstances requiring additional time  
* If the investigation requires more than ten (10) business days, RGRTA is notified in writing with a detailed justification and revised timeline within the initial ten-day period  
* Final written report with findings and recommendations is completed within two (2) business days after investigation conclusion

**3\. Interim Measures**

* The accused employee is immediately removed from the RGRTA assignment upon receipt of the complaint, regardless of the complaint's source  
* The employee is placed on paid administrative leave pending the outcome of the investigation  
* No contact between the accused employee and the complainant is permitted during the investigation  
* A qualified replacement is assigned to the position immediately to ensure uninterrupted service delivery  
* If the complaint involves RGRTA personnel or customers, the employee is reassigned to non-RGRTA duties or placed on leave until resolution

**4\. Communication Protocol**

* RGRTA is notified immediately (within 2 hours) via phone call to the designated RGRTA contact, followed by written confirmation within 4 hours  
* Initial status update is provided to RGRTA within three (3) business days, including investigation scope and expected timeline  
* Weekly status updates are provided to RGRTA every Friday until the investigation is complete  
* Upon conclusion, RGRTA receives a written summary of findings, corrective actions taken, and any personnel changes within two (2) business days  
* All communications are documented and maintained in the complaint file

**5\. Corrective Action Determination Process**

* All sexual harassment complaints are investigated under a zero-tolerance policy in accordance with federal and New York State law  
* Corrective actions are determined based on the severity of the conduct, evidence gathered, and any prior disciplinary history  
* If the complaint is substantiated, corrective action includes immediate termination of employment  
* If the complaint is unsubstantiated but policy violations are identified, appropriate disciplinary action is taken (written warning, mandatory training, or suspension)  
* If the complaint is unsubstantiated and no policy violations are found, the employee may be reinstated with no disciplinary action  
* All corrective actions are documented and communicated to RGRTA

**6\. Reinstatement or Replacement Procedures**

* If the complaint is substantiated, the employee is terminated and permanently removed from RGRTA assignments  
* A qualified replacement is assigned immediately and remains in the position  
* If the complaint is unsubstantiated and the employee is cleared, reinstatement to the RGRTA assignment requires:  
  * Written approval from RGRTA management  
  * Completion of any additional training or counseling as determined by the investigation  
  * A meeting between Utica Security Service management, the employee, and RGRTA to ensure all parties are aligned  
* If RGRTA objects to reinstatement, the employee remains assigned to non-RGRTA duties  
* All reinstatement decisions are documented and communicated to RGRTA in writing

### **b. Physical Altercation**

**1\. Initial Response Timeline**

* All physical altercation complaints are acknowledged within one (1) business day of receipt  
* RGRTA is notified immediately (within 2 hours) upon receipt of any physical altercation complaint  
* Written acknowledgment is provided to the complainant within 24 hours  
* On-site supervisor or management responds immediately to the scene if the incident occurs during duty hours

**2\. Investigation Timeline**

* Initial status update provided to RGRTA within three (3) business days of complaint receipt  
* Full investigation is completed within ten (10) business days, unless coordination with law enforcement extends the timeline  
* If law enforcement is involved, the investigation timeline may be extended, with written notification to RGRTA including justification and revised timeline  
* Final written report with findings and recommendations is completed within two (2) business days after investigation conclusion

**3\. Interim Measures**

* The involved employee(s) are immediately separated from the scene and removed from active duty pending initial assessment  
* If the altercation involves use of force, the employee is placed on paid administrative leave pending investigation  
* If the altercation is between employees, all involved parties are removed from the assignment until the investigation is complete  
* If the altercation involves a member of the public and appears justified under use-of-force policy, the employee may be temporarily reassigned to a different post with increased supervision  
* A qualified replacement is assigned immediately to ensure uninterrupted service delivery  
* Evidence is secured, including witness statements, video footage (if available), and incident reports

**4\. Communication Protocol**

* RGRTA is notified immediately (within 2 hours) via phone call, followed by written confirmation within 4 hours  
* Initial status update is provided to RGRTA within three (3) business days, including preliminary findings and interim measures taken  
* If law enforcement is involved, RGRTA is notified immediately and kept informed of all law enforcement interactions  
* Weekly status updates are provided to RGRTA every Friday until the investigation is complete  
* Upon conclusion, RGRTA receives a written summary of findings, use-of-force compliance review, corrective actions taken, and any personnel changes within two (2) business days  
* All communications are documented and maintained in the complaint file

**5\. Corrective Action Determination Process**

* The investigation reviews all available evidence, including witness statements, video footage, incident reports, and use-of-force policy compliance  
* Corrective actions are determined based on:  
  * Whether the use of force was justified and within policy guidelines  
  * The severity of the altercation and any injuries sustained  
  * Whether proper de-escalation techniques were attempted  
  * The employee's prior disciplinary history  
  * Compliance with reporting and documentation requirements  
* If the altercation was unjustified or violated use-of-force policy, corrective action may include:  
  * Termination for serious violations or repeated offenses  
  * Suspension without pay for moderate violations  
  * Mandatory retraining in de-escalation and use-of-force protocols  
  * Written warning for minor policy violations  
* If the altercation was justified and within policy, no disciplinary action is taken, but additional training may be recommended  
* All corrective actions are documented and communicated to RGRTA

**6\. Reinstatement or Replacement Procedures**

* If the employee is terminated, a qualified replacement is assigned immediately and remains in the position  
* If the employee is suspended, a temporary replacement is assigned for the duration of the suspension  
* If the employee is cleared and reinstated, the process includes:  
  * Written approval from RGRTA management  
  * Completion of any mandatory retraining or counseling  
  * A meeting between Utica Security Service management, the employee, and RGRTA to review expectations  
  * Gradual reintegration with increased supervision for the first 30 days  
* If the employee is reinstated but RGRTA objects, the employee remains assigned to non-RGRTA duties  
* All reinstatement decisions are documented and communicated to RGRTA in writing

### **c. Failure to Perform Duties**

**1\. Initial Response Timeline**

* All complaints regarding failure to perform duties are acknowledged within one (1) business day of receipt  
* RGRTA is notified within 4 hours upon receipt of a complaint, or immediately if the failure poses a safety or security risk  
* Written acknowledgment is provided to the complainant within 24 hours  
* On-site supervisor conducts an initial review within 24 hours of complaint receipt

**2\. Investigation Timeline**

* Initial status update provided to RGRTA within three (3) business days of complaint receipt  
* Full investigation is completed within ten (10) business days for standard performance issues  
* For complex cases involving multiple incidents or patterns of behavior, the investigation may extend to 15 business days with written notification to RGRTA  
* Final written report with findings and recommendations is completed within two (2) business days after investigation conclusion

**3\. Interim Measures**

* The employee remains on duty during investigation unless the failure poses an immediate safety or security risk  
* If immediate risk exists, the employee is temporarily removed from the assignment and replaced  
* Increased supervision is implemented, with daily check-ins and documented performance monitoring  
* The employee may be assigned to a different post or shift with closer supervision  
* Performance improvement plan is initiated if patterns of failure are identified  
* A qualified replacement is on standby if removal becomes necessary

**4\. Communication Protocol**

* RGRTA is notified within 4 hours of complaint receipt (immediately for safety/security risks) via phone call, followed by written confirmation within 8 hours  
* Initial status update is provided to RGRTA within three (3) business days, including preliminary findings and interim measures  
* Weekly status updates are provided to RGRTA every Friday until the investigation is complete  
* Upon conclusion, RGRTA receives a written summary of findings, corrective actions taken, and any personnel changes within two (2) business days  
* If a performance improvement plan is implemented, RGRTA receives monthly updates on progress  
* All communications are documented and maintained in the complaint file

**5\. Corrective Action Determination Process**

* The investigation reviews performance records, incident reports, supervisor observations, and any relevant documentation  
* Corrective actions are determined based on:  
  * The severity and frequency of the performance failures  
  * Whether the failures pose safety or security risks  
  * The employee's prior performance history and disciplinary record  
  * Whether the failures are correctable through training or counseling  
* Progressive discipline is applied as follows:  
  * First offense (minor): Verbal counseling and documented performance improvement plan  
  * Second offense or moderate violation: Written warning and mandatory retraining  
  * Third offense or serious violation: Suspension without pay and comprehensive retraining  
  * Repeated offenses or severe violations: Termination  
* For serious or repeated failures that cannot be corrected, immediate removal and replacement is implemented  
* All corrective actions are documented and communicated to RGRTA

**6\. Reinstatement or Replacement Procedures**

* If the employee is terminated, a qualified replacement is assigned immediately and remains in the position  
* If the employee receives a suspension, a temporary replacement is assigned for the duration  
* If the employee completes corrective action and demonstrates improvement:  
  * Reinstatement requires written approval from RGRTA management  
  * The employee must complete all mandatory retraining and demonstrate competency  
  * A performance improvement plan is implemented with specific, measurable goals  
  * Increased supervision continues for a minimum of 60 days with weekly performance reviews  
  * Monthly progress reports are provided to RGRTA  
* If performance does not improve within 90 days of reinstatement, the employee is removed and replaced  
* If RGRTA objects to reinstatement, the employee remains assigned to non-RGRTA duties or is terminated  
* All reinstatement decisions are documented and communicated to RGRTA in writing

### **d. Unprofessional Conduct**

**1\. Initial Response Timeline**

* All unprofessional conduct complaints are acknowledged within one (1) business day of receipt  
* RGRTA is notified within 4 hours upon receipt of a complaint, or immediately if the conduct poses a risk to public trust or service quality  
* Written acknowledgment is provided to the complainant within 24 hours  
* On-site supervisor conducts an initial review within 24 hours of complaint receipt

**2\. Investigation Timeline**

* Initial status update provided to RGRTA within three (3) business days of complaint receipt  
* Full investigation is completed within ten (10) business days for standard conduct issues  
* For complex cases involving multiple incidents or patterns of behavior, the investigation may extend to 15 business days with written notification to RGRTA  
* Final written report with findings and recommendations is completed within two (2) business days after investigation conclusion

**3\. Interim Measures**

* The employee remains on duty during investigation unless the conduct is severe or poses an immediate risk to public trust  
* If immediate risk exists, the employee is temporarily removed from the assignment and replaced  
* Increased supervision is implemented, with documented monitoring of interactions with the public and RGRTA personnel  
* The employee may be reassigned to a different post or shift to minimize contact with affected parties  
* If the conduct involves RGRTA personnel or customers, the employee is removed from the assignment until resolution  
* A qualified replacement is on standby if removal becomes necessary

**4\. Communication Protocol**

* RGRTA is notified within 4 hours of complaint receipt (immediately for severe conduct) via phone call, followed by written confirmation within 8 hours  
* Initial status update is provided to RGRTA within three (3) business days, including preliminary findings and interim measures  
* Weekly status updates are provided to RGRTA every Friday until the investigation is complete  
* Upon conclusion, RGRTA receives a written summary of findings, corrective actions taken, and any personnel changes within two (2) business days  
* If corrective counseling or training is implemented, RGRTA receives updates upon completion  
* All communications are documented and maintained in the complaint file

**5\. Corrective Action Determination Process**

* The investigation reviews all available evidence, including witness statements, incident reports, and documentation of the conduct  
* Corrective actions are determined based on:  
  * The severity and nature of the unprofessional conduct  
  * Whether the conduct violates contract standards, company policies, or legal requirements  
  * The impact on public trust, service quality, or working relationships  
  * The employee's prior disciplinary history  
  * Whether the conduct is correctable through training or counseling  
* Corrective actions may include:  
  * Termination for severe violations, repeated offenses, or conduct that cannot be corrected  
  * Suspension without pay for moderate violations  
  * Written warning and mandatory training in professional conduct and customer service  
  * Verbal counseling and documented improvement plan for minor violations  
* For conduct that undermines public trust or service quality, immediate removal and replacement is implemented  
* All corrective actions are documented and communicated to RGRTA

**6\. Reinstatement or Replacement Procedures**

* If the employee is terminated, a qualified replacement is assigned immediately and remains in the position  
* If the employee receives a suspension, a temporary replacement is assigned for the duration  
* If the employee completes corrective action and demonstrates improvement:  
  * Reinstatement requires written approval from RGRTA management  
  * The employee must complete all mandatory training in professional conduct and customer service  
  * A conduct improvement plan is implemented with specific behavioral expectations  
  * Increased supervision continues for a minimum of 60 days with documented monitoring  
  * Monthly progress reports are provided to RGRTA  
* If conduct does not improve within 90 days of reinstatement, the employee is removed and replaced  
* If RGRTA objects to reinstatement due to concerns about public trust or service quality, the employee remains assigned to non-RGRTA duties or is terminated  
* All reinstatement decisions are documented and communicated to RGRTA in writing

## **4.4 General Timelines and Expectations**

Utica Security Service, Inc. adheres to the following general timelines for all complaint categories, as expected by RGRTA:

* **Acknowledgment Deadline**: All complaints are acknowledged within one (1) business day of receipt. This includes:  
    
  * Written acknowledgment to the complainant confirming receipt  
  * Internal documentation and case file creation  
  * Assignment of an investigator


* **Initial Status Update Deadline**: An initial status update is provided to RGRTA within three (3) business days of complaint receipt. This update includes:  
  * Preliminary findings from the initial review  
  * Investigation scope and methodology  
  * Expected timeline for completion  
  * Interim measures taken or planned


* **Investigation Completion Deadline**: Full investigations are completed within ten (10) business days of complaint receipt, barring exceptional circumstances. This deadline applies to all complaint categories unless extended in accordance with the written extension procedures below.  
    
* **Written Extension Procedures**: If additional time is required due to case complexity, the following procedures apply:  
  * RGRTA must be notified in writing (email acceptable) with a detailed justification for the extension request  
  * The written notification must be provided within the initial ten (10) business day period, before the deadline expires  
  * The justification must include:  
    * Specific reasons why additional time is needed (e.g., coordination with law enforcement, multiple witnesses requiring interviews, complex evidence review)  
    * The revised timeline with a specific completion date  
    * Interim measures that will remain in place during the extension  
  * RGRTA reserves the right to approve or deny extension requests  
  * If an extension is granted, weekly status updates continue until investigation completion


* **Final Reporting**: Findings and corrective actions are communicated to RGRTA in writing within two (2) business days after investigation conclusion.  
    
* **Acknowledgment of Liquidated Damages**: Utica Security Service, Inc. acknowledges that failure to adhere to these timelines may result in:  
    
  * RGRTA requesting removal of the employee from the contract  
  * Assessment of liquidated damages for unfilled posts during any period of non-compliance  
  * Additional contractual remedies as specified in the contract

Utica Security Service, Inc. is committed to meeting these timelines and will implement internal controls and monitoring to ensure compliance with all deadline requirements.

## **4.5 Interim Measures and Communication Protocols**

Utica Security Service, Inc. maintains comprehensive procedures for implementing interim measures and communicating with RGRTA throughout the complaint investigation process. This section establishes the decision criteria, protocols, and protections that apply across all complaint categories.

### **4.5.1 Decision Criteria for Interim Measures**

Interim measures are determined based on the following decision criteria, which are applied consistently across all complaint categories:

**Risk Assessment Criteria:**

* **Immediate Safety Risk**: If the complaint involves allegations that pose an immediate threat to the safety of the public, RGRTA personnel, or other employees, the employee is immediately removed from the assignment  
* **Public Trust Risk**: If the complaint involves conduct that could undermine public confidence in RGRTA or security services, the employee is removed from public-facing duties  
* **Evidence Preservation**: If the complaint involves potential criminal activity or requires evidence preservation, the employee is removed to prevent interference with the investigation  
* **Complainant Protection**: If the complaint involves a specific complainant (e.g., sexual harassment, threats), the employee is removed to prevent retaliation or further contact  
* **Service Continuity**: If the complaint does not pose immediate risks, the employee may remain on duty with increased supervision to maintain service levels

**Severity-Based Decision Matrix:**

* **High Severity** (Immediate Removal Required):  
  * Sexual harassment complaints  
  * Physical altercations involving use of force  
  * Criminal activity allegations  
  * Threats of violence  
  * Conduct that poses immediate safety risk


* **Moderate Severity** (Removal or Reassignment with Increased Supervision):  
  * Physical altercations that appear justified but require investigation  
  * Repeated performance failures  
  * Unprofessional conduct affecting public trust  
  * Policy violations requiring investigation


* **Low Severity** (Increased Supervision Only):  
  * Single incident of performance failure  
  * Minor unprofessional conduct  
  * First-time policy violations  
  * Correctable performance issues

### **4.5.2 Category-Specific Interim Measures Details**

**a. Sexual Harassment:**

* **Standard Measure**: Immediate removal from RGRTA assignment, placed on paid administrative leave  
* **Rationale**: Zero-tolerance policy requires immediate separation to protect complainant and prevent retaliation  
* **Replacement**: Qualified replacement assigned immediately  
* **Duration**: Until investigation conclusion and final determination

**b. Physical Altercation:**

* **Standard Measure**: Immediate removal from active duty pending initial assessment  
* **Conditional Measure**: If altercation appears justified under use-of-force policy, temporary reassignment to different post with increased supervision  
* **Rationale**: Balance between protecting public safety and preserving service continuity when force may be justified  
* **Replacement**: Qualified replacement assigned immediately if removal is required  
* **Duration**: Until investigation determines whether force was justified and within policy

**c. Failure to Perform Duties:**

* **Standard Measure**: Employee remains on duty with increased supervision unless immediate safety/security risk exists  
* **Conditional Measure**: If failure poses immediate risk, temporary removal and replacement  
* **Rationale**: Performance issues are often correctable; removal only when necessary to protect safety  
* **Replacement**: On standby if removal becomes necessary  
* **Duration**: Until performance improvement is demonstrated or removal is determined necessary

**d. Unprofessional Conduct:**

* **Standard Measure**: Employee remains on duty with increased supervision unless conduct poses immediate risk to public trust  
* **Conditional Measure**: If conduct involves RGRTA personnel or customers, removal from assignment until resolution  
* **Rationale**: Balance between maintaining service levels and protecting public trust  
* **Replacement**: On standby if removal becomes necessary  
* **Duration**: Until conduct is corrected or removal is determined necessary

### **4.5.3 Structured Communication Protocols with Timelines**

Utica Security Service, Inc. maintains structured communication protocols to ensure RGRTA is kept informed throughout the investigation process. All communications follow specific timelines and content requirements.

**Communication Timeline Structure:**

* **Initial Notification** (Within 2-4 hours of complaint receipt, depending on severity):  
  * Phone call to designated RGRTA contact  
  * Written confirmation via email within 4-8 hours (depending on severity)  
  * Content: Complaint category, employee name, brief description, interim measures taken


* **Initial Status Update** (Within 3 business days):  
  * Written status report to designated RGRTA contact  
  * Content: Investigation scope, preliminary findings, interim measures, expected timeline


* **Ongoing Status Updates** (Weekly, every Friday):  
  * Written status report to designated RGRTA contact  
  * Content: Investigation progress, any new developments, interim measures status, revised timeline if applicable


* **Final Report** (Within 2 business days after investigation conclusion):  
  * Written comprehensive report to designated RGRTA contact  
  * Content: Complete findings, evidence summary, corrective actions, personnel changes, reinstatement or replacement decisions


* **Extension Notifications** (Before deadline expiration):  
  * Written notification with justification and revised timeline  
  * Content: Specific reasons for extension, revised completion date, interim measures during extension

### **4.5.4 Designated Contacts**

Utica Security Service, Inc. maintains designated contact points for all complaint-related communications with RGRTA:

**Primary RGRTA Contact:**

* **Role**: Manager of Transit Center Operations (or Field Operations) or designee  
* **Purpose**: Primary point of contact for all complaint notifications and status updates  
* **Contact Method**: Phone (24/7 on-call) and email  
* **Response Time**: Immediate for urgent matters, within 4 hours for standard communications

**Secondary RGRTA Contact:**

* **Role**: Designated RGRTA representative for written communications  
* **Purpose**: Receives all written reports, status updates, and documentation  
* **Contact Method**: Email  
* **Response Time**: Within 1 business day for acknowledgment

**Utica Security Service Contact Structure:**

* **Investigation Manager**: Primary point of contact for all complaint investigations  
* **On-Site Supervisor**: Immediate notification and coordination for on-site incidents  
* **Management Team**: 24/7 availability for urgent matters and escalation

**Contact Information Maintenance:**

* Contact list is maintained and updated as needed  
* RGRTA is notified immediately of any changes to designated contacts  
* Contact information is provided to RGRTA upon contract execution and updated annually or upon request

### **4.5.5 Communication Content Specifications**

All communications with RGRTA follow specific content requirements to ensure consistency and completeness:

**Initial Notification Content:**

* Complaint category (Sexual Harassment, Physical Altercation, Failure to Perform Duties, Unprofessional Conduct)  
* Employee name and assignment location  
* Date and time of complaint receipt  
* Brief description of allegations  
* Source of complaint (RGRTA, public, internal)  
* Interim measures taken or planned  
* Expected timeline for initial status update

**Initial Status Update Content:**

* Complaint reference number and category  
* Investigation scope and methodology  
* Preliminary findings from initial review  
* Interim measures currently in place  
* Expected timeline for investigation completion  
* Any coordination with law enforcement or external agencies  
* Next scheduled update date

**Ongoing Status Update Content:**

* Complaint reference number and category  
* Investigation progress summary  
* Key developments since last update  
* Interim measures status and any changes  
* Revised timeline if applicable  
* Any obstacles or delays encountered  
* Next scheduled update date

**Final Report Content:**

* Complaint reference number and category  
* Complete investigation findings  
* Summary of evidence reviewed (witness statements, documentation, video footage, etc.)  
* Determination (substantiated, unsubstantiated, partially substantiated)  
* Corrective actions taken or planned  
* Personnel changes (removal, replacement, reinstatement)  
* Reinstatement conditions if applicable  
* Any recommendations for policy or procedure improvements

**Extension Request Content:**

* Complaint reference number and category  
* Specific reasons why additional time is needed  
* Detailed justification (e.g., coordination with law enforcement, multiple witnesses, complex evidence review)  
* Revised timeline with specific completion date  
* Interim measures that will remain in place during extension  
* Request for RGRTA approval

### **4.5.6 Confidentiality Protections**

Utica Security Service, Inc. maintains strict confidentiality protections throughout the complaint investigation process to protect all parties while ensuring transparency with RGRTA:

**Confidentiality Principles:**

* All complaint information is treated as confidential and is shared only on a need-to-know basis  
* Employee privacy rights are protected in accordance with applicable federal and New York State laws  
* Complainant confidentiality is maintained unless disclosure is required by law or necessary for investigation  
* Information is shared with RGRTA only to the extent necessary for contract oversight and decision-making

**Information Sharing with RGRTA:**

* RGRTA receives all information necessary for oversight and decision-making regarding personnel assignments  
* Information shared includes: complaint category, investigation status, findings, corrective actions, and personnel decisions  
* Employee personal information (e.g., home address, personal phone numbers) is not shared unless necessary  
* Medical information is shared only with appropriate authorization or as required by law

**Documentation and Recordkeeping:**

* All complaint files are maintained securely with restricted access  
* Electronic files are password-protected and stored on secure servers  
* Physical files are stored in locked cabinets with access limited to authorized personnel  
* Access logs are maintained to track who has viewed complaint files

**Legal Compliance:**

* All confidentiality protections comply with:  
  * Federal privacy laws (e.g., HIPAA where applicable)  
  * New York State labor and privacy laws  
  * Equal Employment Opportunity Commission (EEOC) requirements  
  * Contractual confidentiality requirements

**Breach Procedures:**

* Any unauthorized disclosure of confidential information is immediately reported to management  
* Corrective action is taken to prevent further breaches  
* Affected parties are notified as required by law  
* RGRTA is notified of any breaches that may affect contract oversight

### **4.5.7 Personnel Replacement Capabilities**

Utica Security Service, Inc. maintains a qualified pool of replacement personnel to ensure uninterrupted service delivery during any disciplinary action or investigation. Replacement personnel:

* Meet all contract qualifications and background check requirements  
* Have completed all required training and certifications  
* Are available for immediate assignment with minimal notice  
* Are familiar with RGRTA facilities, post orders, and operational requirements  
* Undergo the same quality assurance and oversight procedures as regular assigned personnel

Replacement assignments are made within 24 hours of a removal decision, or immediately in cases requiring urgent replacement.

## **4.6 Corrective Actions and Personnel Replacement Process**

Utica Security Service, Inc. maintains comprehensive procedures for determining and implementing corrective actions, as well as processes for personnel reinstatement and replacement. This section establishes the decision-making framework, progressive discipline structure, and replacement procedures that ensure accountability while maintaining service continuity.

### **4.6.1 Decision-Making Process for Corrective Actions**

Corrective actions are determined through a structured decision-making process that ensures consistency, fairness, and alignment with contract requirements:

**Investigation Review:**

* All available evidence is reviewed, including:  
  * Witness statements and interviews  
  * Documentation and incident reports  
  * Video footage (if available)  
  * Employee's prior disciplinary history  
  * Performance records and evaluations  
  * Policy compliance review

**Determination Factors:**

* **Severity of the Violation**: The nature and seriousness of the conduct or performance failure  
* **Evidence Strength**: The quality and quantity of evidence supporting the complaint  
* **Prior History**: The employee's disciplinary record and performance history  
* **Mitigating Factors**: Any circumstances that may reduce the severity of the violation  
* **Correctability**: Whether the issue can be addressed through training, counseling, or other corrective measures  
* **Impact Assessment**: The effect on public safety, service quality, public trust, or working relationships

**Decision Authority:**

* **Management Review**: All corrective action decisions are reviewed by senior management  
* **RGRTA Consultation**: For serious violations or when removal is considered, RGRTA is consulted before final determination  
* **Documentation**: All decisions are documented with rationale and supporting evidence

**Appeal Process:**

* Employees have the right to appeal corrective actions through Utica Security Service's internal grievance procedure  
* Appeals do not delay implementation of corrective actions when safety or service continuity is at risk  
* RGRTA is notified of any appeals that may affect personnel assignments

### **4.6.2 Progressive Discipline Structure**

Utica Security Service, Inc. employs a progressive discipline structure that provides opportunities for improvement while ensuring accountability:

**Level 1: Verbal Counseling and Performance Improvement Plan**

* **Applies to**: First-time minor violations, correctable performance issues, minor policy violations  
* **Process**: Supervisor conducts verbal counseling session, documents the issue, and establishes a performance improvement plan with specific, measurable goals  
* **Duration**: Typically 30-60 days with regular check-ins  
* **Outcome**: If improvement is demonstrated, no further action; if not, progression to Level 2

**Level 2: Written Warning and Mandatory Training**

* **Applies to**: Second offense, moderate violations, failure to improve after Level 1, single serious policy violation  
* **Process**: Written warning is issued and placed in employee file, mandatory training or retraining is required, specific improvement expectations are documented  
* **Duration**: Typically 60-90 days with documented monitoring  
* **Outcome**: If improvement is demonstrated, warning remains in file; if not, progression to Level 3

**Level 3: Suspension Without Pay and Comprehensive Retraining**

* **Applies to**: Third offense, serious violations, failure to improve after Level 2, repeated policy violations  
* **Process**: Employee is suspended without pay (typically 1-5 days), comprehensive retraining is required, reinstatement is conditional upon successful completion of retraining and demonstration of competency  
* **Duration**: Suspension period plus 90-day probationary period upon return  
* **Outcome**: If improvement is demonstrated, employee continues with increased supervision; if not, progression to Level 4

**Level 4: Termination**

* **Applies to**: Repeated offenses after Level 3, severe violations that cannot be corrected, conduct that poses immediate safety risk, violations of zero-tolerance policies (e.g., sexual harassment), criminal activity  
* **Process**: Immediate termination, permanent removal from RGRTA assignments, replacement is assigned  
* **Outcome**: Employee is permanently ineligible for RGRTA assignments

**Accelerated Progression:**

* Serious violations may skip levels and proceed directly to appropriate disciplinary action  
* Zero-tolerance violations (e.g., sexual harassment) result in immediate termination regardless of prior history  
* Violations that pose immediate safety risks may result in immediate termination

### **4.6.3 Reinstatement Procedures**

Reinstatement to RGRTA assignments following corrective action requires a structured process to ensure readiness and RGRTA approval:

**Reinstatement Eligibility Criteria:**

* Employee has successfully completed all required corrective actions (training, counseling, suspension period)  
* Employee has demonstrated improvement and competency in required areas  
* No new violations have occurred during the corrective action period  
* Employee is medically and physically fit for duty  
* All required certifications and training are current

**Reinstatement Process:**

* **Step 1: Corrective Action Completion**: Employee completes all required training, counseling, or other corrective measures  
* **Step 2: Competency Verification**: Employee demonstrates competency through testing, evaluation, or observation  
* **Step 3: Management Review**: Utica Security Service management reviews the case and determines readiness for reinstatement  
* **Step 4: RGRTA Notification**: RGRTA is notified in writing of the proposed reinstatement, including:  
  * Summary of the original complaint and corrective actions taken  
  * Evidence of improvement and competency  
  * Proposed reinstatement conditions (if any)  
  * Request for RGRTA approval  
* **Step 5: RGRTA Approval**: Written approval from RGRTA management is required before reinstatement  
* **Step 6: Reinstatement Meeting**: A meeting is conducted between Utica Security Service management, the employee, and RGRTA to:  
  * Review expectations and requirements  
  * Establish any conditions for reinstatement  
  * Ensure all parties are aligned  
* **Step 7: Gradual Reintegration**: Employee is reinstated with increased supervision for a specified period (typically 30-90 days depending on the violation)  
* **Step 8: Ongoing Monitoring**: Regular performance reviews and progress reports are provided to RGRTA

**RGRTA's Final Authority:**

* Utica Security Service, Inc. acknowledges that RGRTA maintains final authority to determine the acceptability or unacceptability of Security staff assigned to perform work under this contract  
* If RGRTA objects to reinstatement, the employee remains assigned to non-RGRTA duties or is terminated, as determined by Utica Security Service management  
* RGRTA's decision regarding reinstatement is final and binding

**Reinstatement Conditions:**

* Increased supervision for specified period  
* Regular performance reviews and reporting to RGRTA  
* Completion of additional training or certification requirements  
* Restrictions on assignments or duties (if applicable)  
* Zero-tolerance policy for any repeat violations

### **4.6.4 Detailed Replacement Process**

When personnel replacement is required, Utica Security Service, Inc. follows a detailed process to ensure qualified replacements are assigned promptly:

**Replacement Triggers:**

* Employee termination  
* Employee suspension exceeding 5 days  
* RGRTA request for removal of unacceptable personnel  
* Employee resignation or inability to perform duties  
* Investigation conclusion requiring permanent removal

**Replacement Timeline:**

* **Standard Replacement**: Replacement is made within one (1) week (7 calendar days) of RGRTA notification or removal decision  
* **Urgent Replacement**: For immediate safety or security risks, replacement is made within 24 hours  
* **Extension**: If replacement cannot be made within one week, RGRTA is notified in writing with justification, and extension must be approved in writing by the Manager of Transit Center Operations or their designee

**Replacement Process Steps:**

* **Step 1: Identification of Need**: Need for replacement is identified through investigation conclusion, RGRTA request, or other trigger  
* **Step 2: Qualification Review**: Replacement pool is reviewed to identify qualified candidates who meet:  
  * All contract qualifications and background check requirements  
  * Required training and certifications  
  * Site-specific requirements (Transit Center vs. Main Street Campus)  
  * Availability for immediate or near-immediate assignment  
* **Step 3: Candidate Selection**: Most qualified candidate is selected based on:  
  * Qualifications and experience  
  * Performance history  
  * Familiarity with RGRTA facilities and post orders  
  * Availability and scheduling compatibility  
* **Step 4: Qualification Verification**: Selected candidate's qualifications are verified:  
  * Background check is current and meets requirements  
  * Training records are reviewed and current  
  * Certifications are valid (NYS Security Guard Certification, DCJS compliance)  
  * Medical and physical fitness standards are met  
  * Drug screening is current (within 60 days)  
* **Step 5: RGRTA Notification**: RGRTA is notified of the proposed replacement, including:  
  * Replacement employee's name and qualifications  
  * Summary of qualifications verification  
  * Proposed start date (within one week)  
  * Request for RGRTA approval (if required)  
* **Step 6: RGRTA Approval**: RGRTA reviews and approves the replacement (RGRTA maintains final authority to determine acceptability)  
* **Step 7: Assignment**: Replacement employee is assigned to the position  
* **Step 8: Orientation**: Replacement employee receives:  
  * Site-specific orientation  
  * Review of post orders  
  * Introduction to RGRTA personnel and procedures  
  * Equipment and facility familiarization  
* **Step 9: Documentation**: All replacement actions are documented and maintained in personnel files

### **4.6.5 Acknowledgment of RGRTA's Final Authority**

Utica Security Service, Inc. fully acknowledges and respects RGRTA's authority regarding personnel assignments:

**RGRTA's Authority:**

* RGRTA reserves the right to require Utica Security Service, Inc. to replace any Security Staff deemed unacceptable to perform work at its facilities under this contract  
* RGRTA maintains final authority to determine, in its sole discretion, the acceptability or unacceptability of Security staff assigned by Utica Security Service, Inc. to perform work under this contract  
* RGRTA's decisions regarding personnel acceptability are final and binding

**Compliance with RGRTA Decisions:**

* Utica Security Service, Inc. will immediately comply with any RGRTA request for personnel replacement  
* Replacement will be made within one (1) week unless extended in writing by the Manager of Transit Center Operations or their designee  
* No employee will be assigned or reinstated to RGRTA facilities without RGRTA approval when such approval is required  
* Utica Security Service, Inc. will not challenge or dispute RGRTA's determinations regarding personnel acceptability

**Communication of RGRTA Authority:**

* All employees assigned to RGRTA facilities are informed of RGRTA's authority regarding personnel assignments  
* Management and supervisory staff are trained on RGRTA's authority and compliance requirements  
* RGRTA's authority is documented in employee handbooks and training materials

### **4.6.6 Qualification Verification for Replacements**

All replacement personnel undergo comprehensive qualification verification to ensure they meet all contract requirements:

**Pre-Assignment Verification Checklist:**

* **Background Check**: Current background check (within required timeframe) verifying:  
  * Three-year prior employer check  
  * Criminal records check for all out-of-state previous employment  
  * Credit check  
  * No disqualifying convictions (felony, sex conviction, dishonorable discharge, etc.)  
* **Licensing and Certification**:  
  * Valid New York State Security Guard Certification (or law enforcement exemption)  
  * Current DOS-issued Photo ID card  
  * Valid Certification of Compliance with DCJS training requirements  
* **Training Verification**:  
  * Eight (8) Hour Pre-Assignment Training completed  
  * Sixteen (16) Hour On-the-Job Training completed (within 90 days of employment)  
  * Eight (8) Hour Annual In-Service Training current (completed within calendar year)  
  * Contract-specific training program completed and approved by RGRTA  
* **Medical and Physical Fitness**:  
  * Medical examination confirming fitness for duty  
  * Physical capability assessment or signed attestation from licensed medical professional  
  * Binocular vision correctable to 20/20  
  * Ability to distinguish colors  
  * Hearing ability (unaided or with aids)  
  * Clear speech  
  * Normal vital signs  
* **Drug Screening**:  
  * Drug test conducted within 60 days prior to assignment  
  * Negative results for all required substances (Cocaine, Opiates, Amphetamines, PCP)  
* **Site-Specific Qualifications** (as applicable):  
  * Transit Center: Advanced Security Guard qualifications (military, law enforcement, or security experience)  
  * Main Street Campus: Security Guard qualifications (1 year security experience or Associate degree)  
* **Employment Eligibility**:  
  * Completed Form I-9 verifying employment eligibility  
  * All required employment documentation

**Verification Process:**

* All qualifications are verified before assignment  
* Verification is documented and maintained in employee files  
* Qualifications are made available to RGRTA upon request  
* Any deficiencies are addressed before assignment

**Ongoing Verification:**

* Qualifications are reviewed annually or upon RGRTA request  
* Expired certifications or training are renewed before expiration  
* Medical and physical fitness standards are verified every two (2) years  
* Random drug testing is conducted in accordance with policy

### **4.6.7 Documentation and Recordkeeping**

All corrective actions, reinstatements, and replacements are fully documented and securely maintained in accordance with applicable laws and contractual requirements. Documentation includes:

* Complaint intake forms with date, time, and source of complaint  
* Written acknowledgments and notifications to RGRTA  
* Investigation reports with evidence, witness statements, and findings  
* Interim measures documentation and rationale  
* Corrective action determinations and implementation records  
* Progressive discipline records (verbal counseling, written warnings, suspensions, terminations)  
* Reinstatement decisions, approvals, and conditions  
* Replacement decisions, qualifications verification, and RGRTA approvals  
* All written communications with RGRTA regarding corrective actions and personnel changes  
* Performance improvement plans and progress reports  
* Qualification verification records for all personnel  
* RGRTA approval documentation for reinstatements and replacements

Records are maintained securely for a minimum of seven (7) years and are available for review by RGRTA upon request, in accordance with confidentiality and legal requirements. All documentation is stored in a manner that protects the privacy of all parties while ensuring accountability and transparency.

# **5\. Performance Metrics Proposal** 

## **5.1 Performance Measurement Approach**

Utica Security Services, Inc. utilizes defined performance metrics to monitor service quality, staffing integrity, supervision, and compliance throughout the RGRTA Security Guard Services (Event 365\) contract. The following key performance indicators (KPIs) will be used to evaluate performance. Metrics are designed to be objective, measurable, and directly tied to service delivery outcomes.

| Performance Metric | Description | Target Standard |
| :---: | :---: | :---: |
| **Post Coverage Rate** | Percentage of scheduled posts fully staffed | **100%** |
| **Unfilled Shift Resolution Time** | Time to deploy replacement staff | **≤ 60 minutes** |
| **Incident Report Timeliness** | Submission of incident reports following events | **Same shift / ≤ 24 hours** |
| **Training Compliance Rate** | Personnel current on required training | **100%** |
| **Supervisor Presence** | Documented supervisory inspections | **Daily / each shift** |
| **Attendance Reliability** | On-time arrival to assigned posts | **≥ 99%** |

 **Performance Review Frequency**  
 Utica Security Services will conduct structured performance reviews to ensure continuous compliance and service improvement.

 • **Weekly internal reviews** will be conducted by the Operations Manager and Site Supervisors to assess coverage, attendance, incidents, and corrective actions.  
 • **Monthly performance summaries** will be provided to RGRTA for review and transparency.  
 • **Quarterly review meetings will be held** with designated RGRTA representatives to evaluate trends, address concerns, and implement improvements as needed.

Utica Security Service, Inc. proposes the following key performance indicators (KPIs):

| KPI | How Measured | How Metricized | Below Expectations | Meet Expectations | Exceed Expectations |
| :---- | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: |
| **Staffing Coverage Rate** | Percentage of scheduled shifts filled | Staffing schedules and attendance records reviewed Biweekly | 95% of scheduled shifts filled | 95% – 99% of scheduled shifts filled | 100% of scheduled shifts filled |
| **Incident Report Timeliness** | Submission of incident reports within required timeframes | Incident reporting system and supervisory review | Reports submitted late or incomplete | Reports submitted within required timeframe | Reports submitted ahead of required deadlines |
| **Training Compliance** | Percentage of assigned personnel current on required training | Training records and compliance reports | \<95% training compliance | 95% – 99% training compliance | 100% training compliance |
| **Supervisor Presence and Inspections** | Frequency of supervisory site visits and inspections | Documented supervisory inspection reports | Inspections not completed as scheduled | Inspections completed as scheduled | Inspections exceed scheduled requirements |
| **Response Time to Incidents** | Time from notification to management response | Incident logs and response documentation | Delayed response beyond acceptable timeframe | Immediate response with documented follow-up | Immediate response with proactive corrective action |
| **Complaint Resolution Timeliness** | Time required to investigate and resolve complaints | Complaint tracking logs and resolution reports | Complaints not resolved within established timelines | Complaints resolved within established timelines | Complaints resolved ahead of established timelines |

## **5.2 Frequency of Review Meetings**

Utica Security Services will provide RGRTA with standardized monthly performance reports that include:  
• Post coverage statistics and attendance records  
 • Summary of incidents and response timelines  
 • Incident report submission compliance  
 • Training and certification compliance status  
 • Supervisor inspection logs and findings  
 • Identified issues and corrective actions taken  
Reports will be formatted for clarity and management review and will be available electronically upon request. These meetings will be used to review metrics, discuss issues, and implement corrective actions when necessary.

## **5.3 Sample Performance Reporting Format**

Performance reports will include:

* Summary of staffing coverage  
* Incident and report statistics  
* Training compliance status  
* Supervisor inspection results  
* Complaint activity and resolution status  
* Corrective actions and improvement plans

Reports will be provided in a clear, organized format and submitted electronically, unless otherwise directed by RGRTA.

**5.4 RGRTA Oversight and Transparency**  
Utica Security Services supports active RGRTA oversight of contract performance. All performance data, reports, and inspection records will be made available to RGRTA upon request. Management remains available to review metrics, discuss trends, and address concerns to ensure continued alignment with RGRTA’s operational and service expectations.

# **6\. Use of Force Policy**

## **6.1 Policy Overview**

Utica Security Service, Inc. maintains a formal Use of Force Policy that governs all armed and unarmed security operations. The policy emphasizes de-escalation, proportional response, and safety while ensuring compliance with applicable federal, New York State, and local laws. The primary objective of the policy is to protect life, prevent injury, and resolve incidents using the least amount of force necessary to achieve lawful objectives. A detailed Standard Operating Procedure (SOP) Governing Use of Force is provided as required supplemental documentation, which contains comprehensive operational procedures, decision-making frameworks, and step-by-step protocols for use-of-force incidents.

## **6.2 Alignment with RGRTA Standards**

Utica Security Service, Inc.'s Use of Force Policy aligns with RGRTA's commitment to:

* Public safety  
* De-escalation and conflict avoidance  
* Professional, respectful, and courteous treatment of all individuals  
* Principles of public service, including cooperation, politeness, and calm demeanor in all interactions  
* Accountability and transparency

The policy clearly defines when force may be used, the types of force authorized, and the circumstances under which escalation is permitted. The policy aligns with RGRTA post orders and operational procedures, including specific requirements for radio notification protocols, escort procedures, and detainment protocols detailed in RGRTA's operational directives.

**Weapons Policy Alignment**  
Utica Security Service, Inc. adheres to RGRTA's weapons policy requirements:

* Security staff shall not carry weapons unless explicitly authorized in writing by both RGRTA and the Vendor  
* Prohibited weapons include, but are not limited to: firearms, tasers, batons, pepper spray/mace, and similar devices  
* Authorized defensive tools (if permitted by contract/post orders) are utilized only by properly trained and licensed personnel and subject to strict policy guidelines and reporting requirements

The Use of Force Policy ensures all personnel operate within the authority permitted by contract, post orders, and applicable law.

**Compliance with Lawful Directives**  
Utica Security Service, Inc.'s Use of Force Policy requires that all security staff adhere to all applicable local ordinances, state and federal laws, RGRTA policies and procedures, and lawful directives issued by law enforcement or RGRTA personnel. Personnel are trained to recognize and comply with lawful directives from authorized officials while operating within the scope of their security authority.

**Post-Order Specific Alignment**  
Utica Security Service, Inc.'s Use of Force Policy aligns with RGRTA post orders requiring that:

* Physical intervention occurs only when reasonably necessary to defend themselves or others from imminent harm or to prevent a crime in progress  
* Only the minimum physical force necessary is used to achieve lawful objectives  
* Security staff employ a proactive rather than reactive approach, intervening early to prevent escalation rather than waiting for situations to become physical

These post-order requirements are integrated throughout the policy and training programs to ensure consistent application across all RGRTA assignments.

**Policy Review and Update Commitment**  
Utica Security Service, Inc. commits to reviewing the Use of Force Policy against RGRTA's use of force guidance (when provided) and updating the policy as necessary to ensure continued alignment with RGRTA standards and expectations. Any updates to the policy will be coordinated with RGRTA and implemented in accordance with contract requirements.

## **6.3 Use of Force Continuum**

The policy incorporates a structured **Use of Force Continuum**, which includes:

1. **Officer Presence and Verbal Commands**  
   * Professional appearance and clear communication  
   * Attempted voluntary compliance  
   * Verbal direction and commands with calm tone

   

2. **De-Escalation Techniques**  
   * Verbal persuasion and active listening  
   * Crisis intervention strategies  
   * Time, distance, and positioning  
   * Management of aggressive behaviors through de-escalation as a means of self-defense and to protect others from bodily harm

   

3. **Defensive Control Techniques**  
   * Physical control techniques used only when necessary  
   * Focus on stopping behavior and minimizing injury  
   * Use only when verbal de-escalation fails and an imminent safety threat exists  
   * Minimum force necessary to cease unsafe conduct and regain control

   

4. **Authorized Defensive Tools (Armed Assignments Only)**  
   * Utilized only by properly trained and licensed personnel  
   * Subject to strict policy guidelines and reporting requirements  
   * Only if permitted by contract/post orders and officer qualifications

Deadly force is used only as a last resort and strictly in accordance with law. The policy emphasizes a proactive versus reactive approach, with security staff intervening early to prevent escalation rather than waiting for situations to become physical.

## **6.4 Staffing and Response Time Capabilities**

Utica Security Service, Inc. ensures that:

* Armed posts are staffed only with properly trained and licensed officers  
* Supervisors are available to respond to incidents on-site within 15 minutes during operational hours  
* Management is on call 24/7 for escalation support with prompt response commitments  
* Site Supervisors serve as primary on-call contacts for immediate response to use-of-force incidents

**Management Availability to RGRTA**  
Utica Security Service, Inc. 's management team is on-call to RGRTA and Security staff twenty-four (24) hours per day, seven days per week, enabling RGRTA and Security staff to discuss issues, events, or questions in real-time. A contact list will be provided to RGRTA and updated as needed and/or upon request.

**Response Time Commitments**

* Site Supervisor responds on-site to use-of-force incidents within 15 minutes during operational hours  
* Management escalation contact available 24/7 with commitment to respond to critical incidents within 1 hour  
* RGRTA notified within 1 hour of any use-of-force incident requiring management review

Supervisory and management personnel respond promptly to any use-of-force incident to ensure control, documentation, and compliance review.

## **6.5 Incident Reporting and Review**

All use-of-force incidents require:

* Immediate notification to supervisory staff  
* Detailed written incident reports (by end of shift or within 12 hours maximum)  
* Management review for policy compliance (within 24 hours of receipt)  
* RGRTA notification: Initial notification within 1 hour for incidents requiring management review; detailed summary provided within 1–2 business days (or sooner for serious incidents)

Use-of-force incidents are analyzed for trends, training needs, and potential policy updates.

**911/Law Enforcement/EMS Requirements:** Officers shall request law enforcement and/or EMS immediately when: a weapon is present or suspected; any party is injured or requests medical attention; any force results in visible injury, head impact, loss of consciousness, or distress; assault, credible threat of violence, or serious disorder occurs; or the situation cannot be controlled safely with de-escalation and minimal defensive measures. (See SOP Section 10.2 for complete details.)

## **6.6 Training and Supervision**

Utica Security Service, Inc. provides comprehensive training aligned with RGRTA contract-specific standards, including:

* Initial and annual training on use-of-force policies  
* Hands-on and scenario-based use of force, de-escalation, and management of aggressive behaviors training as a means of self-defense and to protect others from bodily harm  
* Annual 8-hour use-of-force refresher training, including 4 hours of scenario-based de-escalation exercises  
* Training on RGRTA post-order specific procedures, including radio notification protocols, escort procedures, and detainment protocols  
* Supervisory oversight of all armed assignments  
* Remedial training following any use-of-force incident, when necessary

Training covers operational procedures specific to RGRTA post orders, including:

* Radio notification requirements before physical intervention (including "1128 at Gate \#" protocol at Transit Center)  
* 2-officer minimum escort procedures  
* Camera coverage coordination with Radio Control  
* Verbal order requirements before physical force  
* Detainment procedures, including pat down protocols, 2:1 escort requirements, and same-sex presence requirements

Competency is verified through written examination and practical scenario evaluation. Detailed training curriculum and instructional hours are specified in Section 3: Training Plan.Only personnel who have completed required training and demonstrated competency are assigned to posts where force may be authorized.

## **6.7 Remediation Plan for Staffing Deficiencies**

In the event of staffing or performance deficiencies related to use-of-force compliance:

* Personnel may be immediately removed from duty upon identification of compliance failure  
* Replacement officers are deployed within 4 hours to ensure service continuity  
* Retraining or disciplinary action is implemented as appropriate  
* RGRTA is informed of corrective actions taken within 1 hour of personnel removal

**Timeline Commitments**

* Personnel removal: Immediate upon identification of use-of-force policy compliance failure  
* Replacement deployment: Within 4 hours to ensure no service interruption  
* RGRTA notification: Within 1 hour of personnel removal or corrective action implementation  
* Remediation integration: Corrective actions tracked and monitored through Quality Assurance oversight program (Section 8\) and staffing processes (Section 2). 

Financial accountability measures apply per contract terms for unresolved staffing deficiencies. This approach ensures continued compliance and protection of public safety.

## **6.8 Operational Procedures Alignment**

Utica Security Service, Inc.'s Use of Force Policy aligns with RGRTA post-order specific operational procedures:  
**Escalation Procedures**

* Initial warning provided before requesting compliance  
* Clear verbal direction and request to leave if misconduct continues  
* Backup requested and minimum 2-officer escort required for non-compliant individuals  
* Radio Control contacted before initiating any escort to ensure camera coverage  
* All escorts proceed to nearest exit as directed by post orders

**Physical Altercation Response**

* Verbal order to cease behavior is required before any physical intervention  
* Radio notification ("1128 at Gate \#" at Transit Center) must be made prior to engaging in physical altercation  
* Only minimum physical force necessary to cease fighting and detain individuals using Vendor-issued handcuffs  
* Emphasis on proactive intervention to prevent escalation rather than reactive response

**Detainment Procedures**

* Pat down individuals placed in handcuffs for safety purposes only  
* 2:1 escorts required for individuals detained in handcuffs  
* Security staff of same sex as detainee must be present whenever possible  
* Detainees are not to be left until properly relieved by another Security Staff or Police Officer

These operational procedures are detailed in the Standard Operating Procedure Governing Use of Force provided as required supplemental documentation. The policy emphasizes that physical confrontation is exceptional and only used when reasonably necessary to defend themselves or others from imminent harm or to prevent a crime in progress, consistent with RGRTA post orders.
