# **7\. Background Check and Drug Testing Procedures** 

## **Overview**

Utica Security Service, Inc. maintains comprehensive background screening and drug testing procedures to ensure that all personnel assigned to RGRTA facilities are qualified, trustworthy, and compliant with applicable federal, New York State, and local labor laws. These procedures are applied consistently and fairly across all employment categories. 

## **7.1 Pre-Employment and Random Drug Testing Protocols** 

All prospective employees undergo a thorough pre-employment background screening process prior to assignment, which includes:

* Criminal history background checks conducted in accordance with New York State and federal law  
* Verification of identity and employment eligibility  
* Drug test  
* Employment history verification  
* License and certification verification, including NYS armed guard credentials where applicable

Only individuals who meet all screening criteria and regulatory requirements are permitted to work on RGRTA assignments. All security staff assigned to RGRTA will complete a pre-assignment drug test within sixty (60) days prior to initial assignment, at Utica Security’s expense, using urine or oral fluid (mouth swab) collection. Testing will screen, at minimum, for cocaine, opiates, amphetamines (including methamphetamine), and PCP using SAMHSA-standardized cut-off levels required by RGRTA. 

**Reasonable Suspicion Testing:** Utica Security will comply with RGRTA requests for drug and/or alcohol testing when there is reasonable suspicion of impairment. If a test result is positive, Utica Security will bear the cost; if negative, RGRTA will reimburse the testing cost. Where specific, articulable symptoms of cannabis impairment are observed affecting performance or workplace safety, Utica Security will support RGRTA-requested cannabis testing consistent with applicable law and RGRTA requirements.

**Random Drug Testing:** Utica Security will conduct **random drug testing** of security staff assigned to the RGRTA contract at Utica Security’s expense. The **Random Drug Testing Policy and Procedure** is provided as a **standalone document** and will be incorporated into the final contract upon mutual agreement; any changes will require RGRTA written approval. Utica Security Service, Inc. enforces a drug-free workplace policy that includes:

* **Pre-employment drug testing** for all security personnel  
* **Random drug testing** conducted periodically in accordance with applicable laws  
* **Reasonable suspicion testing** when behavior or incidents indicate potential impairment  
* **Post-incident testing** following serious incidents or use-of-force events, when appropriate

Testing is conducted by certified laboratories, and results are handled in a confidential manner. 

**7.2 Background check procedures**  
Utica Security will conduct background checks for all personnel assigned to RGRTA at Utica Security’s expense and will provide confirmation documentation to RGRTA as required. Background screening will include, at minimum:   
(1) a three-year prior employer check.  
(2) criminal records checks for all out-of-state previous employment; and   
(3) a credit check.   
Personnel will be disqualified in accordance with RGRTA standards (including felony/sex convictions, dishonorable discharge, and patterns of irresponsible behavior).

## **Background Check Process**

Utica Security Services, Inc. administers a structured, multi-step background screening process for all personnel assigned to the RGRTA Security Guard Services (Event 365\) contract. All background checks are conducted at Utica Security’s expense and completed prior to assignment. The background screening process includes the following steps:

1. **Employment History Verification**  
   A verification of the applicant’s prior three (3) years of employment is conducted to confirm work history, reliability, and suitability for assignment in a public-facing transit environment.  
2. **Criminal Records Screening**  
   Criminal history checks are performed for all jurisdictions corresponding to prior out-of-state employment, in addition to standard screening required under New York State regulations.  
3. **Credit History Review**  
   A credit check is conducted as part of the suitability assessment to identify potential risk factors relevant to positions of trust and responsibility.  
4. **Disqualification Review**  
   Screening results are reviewed against RGRTA’s disqualifying standards. Personnel are disqualified from assignment if they have a history of felony or sex-related convictions, military discharge under dishonorable or undesirable conditions, or a documented pattern of irresponsible behavior, including unsafe driving or problematic employment history.  
5. **Documentation & Verification**  
   Confirmation of completed background screening is maintained in the employee’s secure personnel file. Verification documentation will be made available to RGRTA upon request, consistent with confidentiality and applicable labor laws.   
   This process ensures that only qualified, vetted, and suitable personnel are assigned to RGRTA facilities, supporting public safety, operational integrity, and compliance with RGRTA’s contractual requirements.

## **7.3 Compliance with Federal and New York State Labor Laws**

All background checks and drug testing procedures are conducted in compliance with:

* Fair Credit Reporting Act (FCRA)  
* New York State labor and human rights laws  
* Applicable local regulations

Utica Security Service, Inc. ensures that screening practices are lawful, job-related, and consistent with business necessity.

**Non-Discrimination and Confidentiality Safeguards**Utica Security Service, Inc. is committed to equal employment opportunity and non-discrimination. Screening and testing procedures are applied uniformly without regard to race, color, religion, sex, age, disability, or other protected characteristics.

 **Legal Compliance, Non-Discrimination & Confidentiality:** All drug testing and background screening will be administered in a **non-discriminatory, confidential** manner consistent with applicable federal and New York State labor laws. Screening results will be maintained securely with access limited to authorized management and released to RGRTA only as required for contract compliance. All background and drug testing records are:

* Securely maintained  
* Access-restricted to authorized personnel  
* Used solely for employment and compliance purposes.

Confidentiality is strictly enforced.

## **7.4 Responsiveness to RGRTA Standards**

Utica Security Service, Inc. is prepared to:

* Adjust screening protocols to align with RGRTA requirements  
* Provide confirmation of compliance upon request  
* Immediately remove and replace any personnel who fail to meet screening standards

This ensures ongoing compliance and protection of RGRTA’s operational and public safety interests.

# **8\. Quality Assurance and Oversight**

## **8.1 Quality Assurance Program Overview**

Utica Security Service, Inc. maintains a comprehensive Quality Assurance (QA) and oversight program designed to ensure consistent service delivery, regulatory compliance, and continuous improvement. The QA program focuses on proactive monitoring, documented inspections, and responsive corrective action to maintain high performance standards across all assigned RGRTA facilities.

**8.2 Weekly Inspection Procedures**  
Field agents conduct weekly on-site inspections to verify compliance with post orders, company policies, and RGRTA requirements. Inspections include, but are not limited to:

* Verification of officer presence and punctuality  
* Uniform and appearance compliance  
* Adherence to post orders and patrol requirements  
* Review of logbooks and incident reports  
* Observation of officer interaction with the public  
* Assessment of situational awareness and professionalism

**Minimum Inspection Frequency**  
At a minimum, each RGRTA facility and post assignment will receive one (1) documented inspection per week, with additional random spot inspections conducted as operational conditions warrant or when performance concerns are identified.

**Documentation and Reporting Tools**  
All inspections are documented using a formal Weekly Site Inspection Checklist and recorded within Utica Security Service, Inc, workforce management and reporting systems (e.g., Connecteam / internal QA log). Each inspection record includes the inspection date and time, site and post location, inspector name, compliance findings, deficiencies (if any), corrective actions, responsible party, and closure status. Inspection records are retained and made available to RGRTA upon request.

## **8.3 Monthly Inspection Log Reporting**

Utica Security Service, Inc. prepares Monthly Inspection Log Reports that summarize all weekly inspection activities, compliance findings, and corrective actions for the preceding calendar month. These reports support transparency, accountability, and continuous improvement throughout the contract term.

**Report Format and Delivery**  
Monthly Inspection Log Reports are provided to RGRTA in two complementary formats:

* **Primary Format:** PDF document with executive summary, detailed findings, and supporting data visualization  
* **Secondary Format:** Microsoft Excel workbook (.xlsx) containing raw data tables for RGRTA analysis and reporting

Reports are delivered electronically via secure email to designated RGRTA representatives, including the Manager of Transit Center Operations (or Field Operations) and designated contract administrators. Alternative delivery methods may be utilized upon RGRTA request.

**Delivery Deadline**  
Monthly Inspection Log Reports are provided at the end of each month, and in all cases no later than the 5th business day of the following month. This timeline ensures data accuracy and completeness while providing RGRTA with timely information for performance review and operational planning.

**Report Content**  
Each Monthly Inspection Log Report includes:

**Executive Summary**

* Overview of reporting period and inspection coverage  
* Overall compliance rate and high-level performance metrics  
* Summary of critical findings and immediate corrective actions

**Inspection Statistics**

* Total inspections completed (scheduled and random spot inspections)  
* Breakdown by facility, post assignment, and inspector  
* Inspection coverage percentage and completion rate  
* Comparison of scheduled vs. completed inspections

**Compliance Findings**

* Compliance status by category (officer presence, uniform, post orders, conduct, documentation)  
* Overall compliance rate percentage  
* Deficiency count and categorization by severity (minor, moderate, critical)  
* Summary of non-compliance trends and patterns

**Deficiencies and Corrective Actions**

* Complete listing of deficiencies identified, including description, location, date, and severity  
* Corrective actions implemented with tracking and closure status  
* Deficiencies requiring follow-up inspection or monitoring

**Supporting Documentation**

* Weekly Site Inspection Checklist summaries (organized by facility and date)  
* Corrective Action Tracking Log  
* Additional documentation as applicable

**Quality Assurance**  
Prior to submission, each Monthly Inspection Log Report undergoes data verification, accuracy review, and compliance check against contract requirements. Reports are compiled from Utica Security Service, Inc  workforce management and reporting systems, including Connecteam, internal QA logs, daily Security Officer Reports, and incident reporting systems.  
This structured reporting framework ensures RGRTA receives comprehensive, accurate, and actionable information to support performance evaluation and operational decision-making.

## **8.4 Internal Compliance Monitoring**

In addition to routine field inspections, Utica Security Service, Inc. maintains a structured internal compliance monitoring program to ensure all personnel assigned to the RGRTA contract meet regulatory, contractual, and operational requirements.

**8.4.1 Monitoring Frequency**  
Internal compliance reviews are conducted on the following schedule:

* **Daily:** Shift hour limit monitoring, scheduling exception review  
* **Weekly:** Attendance and shift coverage audits, overtime reviews  
* **Monthly:** Comprehensive training records review, licensing status verification, incident and use-of-force report analysis  
* **Quarterly:** Full personnel file audits, performance metric trend analysis, policy compliance assessments, background check and drug testing compliance review

**8.4.2 Monitoring Methodology and Tools**  
Internal compliance monitoring utilizes a combination of automated systems and manual review processes:

* **Automated Monitoring:** Utica Security Service, Inc workforce management system (Connecteam) provides automated alerts for training expiration dates, licensing renewals, shift hour threshold warnings, and scheduling conflicts. The system generates exception reports flagging potential compliance issues for supervisor review.  
* **Manual Reviews:** Supervisors conduct structured file audits using standardized compliance checklists. Manual reviews include verification of documentation accuracy, cross-referencing of records, and quality checks on automated system outputs.  
* **Audit Procedures:** Quarterly personnel file audits follow a systematic review protocol covering all compliance categories. Audit findings are documented using a standardized Compliance Audit Report form.

**8.4.3 Monitoring Responsibility and Accountability**

| Monitoring Activity | Responsible Party | Review Authority | Frequency |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| Shift hour limit monitoring | Site Supervisor | Operations Manager | Daily |
| Attendance/coverage audits | Site Supervisor | Operations Manager | Weekly |
| Training records review | Operations Manager | Contract Administrator | Monthly |
| Licensing status verification | Operations Manager | Contract Administrator | Monthly |
| Background check currency | Contract Administrator | Executive Management | Quarterly |
| Drug testing compliance | Contract Administrator | Executive Management | Quarterly |
| Full personnel file audit | Contract Administrator | Executive Management | Quarterly |

**8.4.4 Specific Compliance Checks**  
The internal compliance monitoring program includes verification of the following regulatory and contractual requirements:

1. **NYS Department of State (DOS) and Division of Criminal Justice Services (DCJS) Training Compliance**  
* Verification that all personnel have completed Eight (8) Hour Pre-Assignment Training prior to duty assignment  
* Tracking and confirmation of Sixteen (16) Hour On-the-Job Training completion within 90 days of employment  
* Monitoring of Eight (8) Hour Annual In-Service Training completion for each calendar year  
* Maintenance of current DOS-issued Photo ID cards and DCJS Certificates of Compliance for all assigned personnel  
* 30-day, 60-day, and 90-day training deadline alerts to prevent compliance lapses

2. **Employment Eligibility Verification**  
* Maintenance of completed Form I-9 for each security staff member, verifying employment eligibility in accordance with federal law  
* I-9 documentation review during onboarding and periodic audits to ensure records remain current and accessible upon RGRTA request

3. **Shift Hour Limit Compliance**  
* Daily monitoring to ensure no security staff works more than sixteen (16) hours in any twenty-four (24) hour period, except in the limited circumstances permitted under the contract (government-issued travel advisory or state of emergency restricting travel)  
* Scheduling system alerts and supervisor review protocols to prevent shift limit violations before they occur  
* Documentation and reporting of any shift limit exceptions, including justification and corrective measures

4. **Background Check and Drug Testing Compliance Monitoring**  
* Verification that all personnel have completed required background checks (three-year employer check, criminal records check for out-of-state employment, credit check) prior to assignment  
* Tracking of background check currency and re-verification requirements as applicable  
* Monitoring of random drug testing program compliance, ensuring testing frequency meets policy requirements  
* Documentation of all drug test results and maintenance of chain-of-custody records  
* Tracking of reasonable suspicion testing requests and outcomes  
* Verification that pre-employment drug tests were completed within sixty (60) days prior to initial assignment

5. **Equipment Compliance Monitoring**  
* Verification that all personnel are issued required vendor-supplied equipment (handcuffs, case, glove pouch) prior to assignment  
* Tracking of equipment condition and replacement needs  
* Monitoring of RGRTA-supplied equipment sign-out/sign-in compliance (radios, ID badges)  
* Documentation of equipment damage or loss incidents for accountability tracking  
* Periodic inventory audits to ensure equipment accountability

**8.4.5 Documentation and Recordkeeping**  
All internal compliance monitoring activities are documented and maintained within Utica Security Service, Inc workforce management and compliance tracking systems. Records include:

* Compliance review date and reviewer name  
* Personnel reviewed and compliance status  
* Deficiencies identified with severity classification  
* Corrective actions assigned with responsible party and deadline  
* Follow-up verification and closure documentation

Compliance records are retained for the duration of the contract and made available to RGRTA upon request.

**Additional Compliance Areas**

* Incident and use-of-force report reviews  
* Performance metric analysis  
* Medical and physical fitness certification currency (biennial review)

**8.4.6 Integration with Monthly Reporting**  
Internal compliance monitoring results are integrated into the Monthly Inspection Log Report provided to RGRTA. The Monthly Internal Compliance Summary section of the report includes:

* Summary of compliance monitoring activities completed during the reporting period  
* Compliance status by category (training, licensing, background, drug testing, equipment, shift limits)  
* Deficiencies identified and corrective actions taken  
* Personnel compliance rates and trending analysis identifying patterns or recurring issues  
* Upcoming compliance deadlines (training renewals, licensing expirations) for the following month

**Sample Internal Compliance Monitoring Checklist**  
The following checklist framework is used during personnel compliance audits:

| Compliance Category | Check Item | Status |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Licensing** | DOS Photo ID current and on file | ☐ Compliant / ☐ Deficient |
|  | DCJS Certificate of Compliance current | ☐ Compliant / ☐ Deficient |
|  | Security Guard Registration valid | ☐ Compliant / ☐ Deficient |
| **Training** | 8-Hr Pre-Assignment Training completed | ☐ Compliant / ☐ Deficient |
|  | 16-Hr OJT completed (within 90 days) | ☐ Compliant / ☐ Deficient |
|  | 8-Hr Annual In-Service Training current | ☐ Compliant / ☐ Deficient |
|  | Contract-specific training completed | ☐ Compliant / ☐ Deficient |
| **Employment** | Form I-9 on file and current | ☐ Compliant / ☐ Deficient |
|  | Background check completed | ☐ Compliant / ☐ Deficient |
| **Drug Testing** | Pre-employment test completed (within 60 days) | ☐ Compliant / ☐ Deficient |
|  | Random testing compliance current | ☐ Compliant / ☐ Deficient |
| **Medical/Physical** | Medical exam current (initial/biennial) | ☐ Compliant / ☐ Deficient |
|  | Physical fitness certification current | ☐ Compliant / ☐ Deficient |
| **Equipment** | Vendor-supplied equipment issued | ☐ Compliant / ☐ Deficient |
|  | RGRTA ID badge active | ☐ Compliant / ☐ Deficient |
| **Shift Compliance** | No 16-hr limit violations | ☐ Compliant / ☐ Deficient |

Completed checklists are retained in personnel files and summarized in quarterly compliance audit reports. These reviews ensure continued compliance with contractual obligations, regulatory standards, and RGRTA expectations.

**8.5 On-Call Management and Escalation Structure**  
Utica Security Service, Inc. will provide 24/7/365 on-call management availability through a structured, two-tier escalation model utilizing a dedicated Site Supervisor and an Operations Manager assigned to the RGRTA contract.

* **Tier 1 \* Site Supervisor (Primary On-Call):**  
  A designated Site Supervisor will serve as the primary on-call contact for RGRTA and on-duty security personnel. The Site Supervisor will be responsible for immediate response to staffing deficiencies, routine incidents, and operational concerns, including on-site deployment when required. The Site Supervisor will acknowledge all urgent calls promptly and initiate corrective actions without delay.  
* **Tier 2 \* Operations Manager (Secondary Escalation):**  
  The Operations Manager will serve as the secondary escalation authority and will be available 24/7 to address critical incidents, repeated staffing issues, policy deviations, or matters requiring management-level decision-making. The Operations Manager has full authority to deploy additional resources, authorize coverage adjustments, implement corrective actions, and coordinate directly with RGRTA leadership as needed.  
* **Escalation and Documentation:**  
  RGRTA will be provided with a direct escalation contact list identifying the Site Supervisor (primary) and Operations Manager (secondary). All after-hours escalations, responses, and resolutions will be documented, logged, and reviewed by management to ensure timely resolution, accountability, and identification of operational trends.

## **8.6 Corrective Action and Continuous Improvement**

When deficiencies or performance concerns are identified through inspections, compliance monitoring, or RGRTA feedback, Utica Security Service, Inc. implements a structured corrective action process based on severity classification.

**Severity-Based Response Framework**

| Severity Level | Examples | Response Timeline | Corrective Action |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Critical** | Expired license, missing background check, shift limit violation, working without required training | Immediate (same day) | Personnel removed from duty pending resolution; RGRTA notified within 24 hours; root cause analysis initiated |
| **Moderate** | Training deadline approaching (\<30 days), documentation incomplete, minor policy deviation | Within 48 hours | Supervisor follow-up, compliance plan established with target date, monitored until resolved |
| **Minor** | Administrative errors, minor documentation gaps, equipment condition issues | Within 5 business days | Correction implemented, retraining or coaching as needed |

1. **Corrective Action Process**  
1. **Identification:** Deficiency identified through inspection, compliance monitoring, or report  
2. **Classification:** Severity level assigned based on impact and regulatory implications  
3. **Notification:** RGRTA notified of critical issues within 24 hours; moderate/minor issues included in regular reporting  
4. **Assignment:** Corrective action assigned to responsible party with target completion date  
5. **Implementation:** Corrective measures executed (retraining, policy update, personnel action, etc.)  
6. **Verification:** Follow-up review confirms resolution and prevents recurrence  
7. **Closure:** Documentation completed and filed; trends analyzed for systemic improvement

**b. Corrective Action Types**

* Additional supervision or inspections  
* Targeted retraining or coaching  
* Counseling and progressive discipline  
* Personnel reassignment or replacement when necessary  
* Policy or procedural updates  
* System or process improvements

**c. Tracking and Accountability**  
All corrective actions are documented in the Corrective Action Tracking Log, which includes:

* Date identified and description of deficiency  
* Severity classification and responsible party  
* Corrective action required and target completion date  
* Status updates and verification of completion  
* Closure date and preventive measures implemented

Unresolved or recurring compliance issues are escalated to the Operations Manager and, if necessary, reported to RGRTA with a remediation plan. Corrective action trends are reviewed monthly to identify systemic issues and drive continuous improvement initiatives.

## **8.7 Integration with RGRTA Oversight**

Utica Security Service, Inc. works collaboratively with RGRTA to align quality assurance efforts with the Authority’s oversight expectations. This includes:

* Open communication with designated RGRTA representatives  
* Participation in scheduled performance review meetings  
* Responsiveness to RGRTA feedback and directives  
* Immediate escalation of critical issues

This collaborative approach ensures alignment with RGRTA’s operational priorities and service standards.

## **8.8 Accountability and Reporting Transparency**

Utica Security Service, Inc. is committed to transparency and accountability. All QA documentation, inspection reports, and corrective action records are maintained and made available to RGRTA upon request. This structured oversight framework ensures dependable service delivery, rapid issue resolution, and continuous improvement throughout the contract term.
